# TUI inventory: goodvibes-tui to products/tui and packages/engine

Every tracked file of goodvibes-tui (1618 files, from `git -C ~/Projects/goodvibes-tui ls-files`), read in full, with its disposition and, for a JEV or HOIST file, its decision points. Nothing is dropped unless it is specific to WRFC (the old write, review, fix, confirm chain: workstream orchestration, attempt judging, the WRFC panel format, WRFC persistence, the WRFC agent guard) or specific to QEMU sandboxing. The intent document's other DROP calls for this product are void per the owner ruling of 2026-09-26: the planning, work-plan, workstream, review and codebase command runtimes it names as DROP carry forward instead, either as JEV onto the contract runner or as plain PORT, once read and confirmed to hold no WRFC or QEMU content, matching the precedent already set for goodvibes-daemon's `legacy-daemon-migration.ts` and `legacy-daemon-reconcile.ts` in docs/inventory/daemon.md. TUI views of WRFC-era state (chain panels, review summaries, workstream views, and the workstream notification) become views or notifications of the contract tree: these are JEV, noted as such, never DROP and never a plain PORT, since the screen looks the same but the data model underneath is now the contract tree. Permission mode, the eval registry, cost tracking, the user error formatter, natural-language schedule parsing, payment card intake, the recall commands, policy and policy dispatch, and a handful of other files the intent document marks HOIST elsewhere in its platform tables turn up here too, on the product side of that same move, named against the engine subsystem they land in. The UI is not redesigned: every screen, panel, command, key binding, style and interaction matches the old product exactly. docs/inventory/daemon.md and docs/inventory/agent.md are the matching inventories for the sibling daemon and agent products, docs/inventory/engine.md is the matching inventory for packages/engine (from goodvibes-sdk), and docs/inventory/wrfc-to-jev.md maps every WRFC function to its Jev form.

Read, understand, choose (owner ruling 2026-09-27): each row names every check its file makes (each list, set, regex, pattern table, cutoff or threshold, lookup, comparison or classification whose result decides what the code does next), with its line, what it decides and how it decides it today, and the choice made on that reading. A check that answers a question of meaning with a guess becomes a Jev reading, named by pattern and battery; a check stays code only with the concrete reason it is not a guess (id equality, arithmetic, a size or limit the program sets, a grammar a machine produced, a closed enum this program writes, a key event compared to a configured binding, a value the owner configured, exactly what the user typed). A category label (deterministic, fixed format, security, display only, renders already-judged state) is never the reason to keep a check as code, and a blanket conversion is never the reason to make one a Jev reading: security checks, boundary lists, taint, trust tiers, sanitizers, redaction and format detection get the same reading as everything else. Where a check's engine counterpart is already decided in docs/inventory/engine.md, the row cites that engine row. No heuristic is kept behind a Jev reading, not even as a backup, and what a site does when Jev cannot answer is left to the owner.

Correction (coordinator, 2026-09-27): WRFC code is JEV in the contract runner, as in the engine inventory, so the WRFC guard, panel format, persistence, reviewer rubric and their tests are JEV below; DROP is kept only for the three QEMU files and the literal WRFC run artifact with its test.

Files: 1618. PORT 1440, JEV 89, HOIST 84, DROP 5. Decision points: 27.

Directly asserts the `autoSubmit`-true and `autoSubmit`-false branches of `wake-runtime.ts`'s transcribeUtterance (lines ~500-522 and ~457-498), so it follows that file's JEV disposition: once auto-submit consults the advisory gate battery, this test's autoSubmit-on case is updated to stub/assert the battery's yes/no outcome rather than a bare boolean flag. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/audio/wake-runtime.ts:200` (`if (settings.autoSubmit) deps.submitTurn(text); else { deps.writeDraft(text); ... }`) | A single static per-user config toggle (`voice.wake.autoSubmit`) decides, with no per-utterance judgment, whether every speech-to-text transcript that follows a wake is submitted straight into the orchestrator as a live command turn, or held in the composer for the user to review; a misrecognized or unintended utterance is acted on exactly as readily as a deliberate one. | Gate subsystem's sandbox-advisory battery (yes/no): is this recognized post-wake utterance safe and sensible to auto-submit as a turn without user review; a "no" (or a battery that cannot be reached) falls back to the existing `writeDraft` composer path rather than ever failing open. |

## Providers, shell and verification

| `src/providers/provider-classification.ts` | HOIST | Engine subsystem: providers. `classifyProviderSetup` maps a provider to its billing class (api-key/cloud-account/local/subscription/self-hosted/no-key-free/unknown) so the failover notice can name what it switched onto. Named explicitly in intent's TUI HOIST bullets ("provider classification, provider fallback, provider health"). Checked for a decision point per the slice notes: it is purely structural/enum-based, exact membership in fixed provider-id sets (`SUBSCRIPTION_PROVIDERS`, `LOCAL_PROVIDERS`, `SELF_HOSTED_PROVIDERS`, `CLOUD_ACCOUNT_PROVIDERS`) plus exact-string comparisons against a closed set of known `authMode` enum values (`'oauth'`, `'api-key'`, `'bearer'`, `'anonymous'`, `'none'`). No free text is parsed or fuzzy-matched, so there is no decision point here; it hoists as plain code. |

No free-text-to-effort-tier guesswork lives here, so it carries over unchanged, only its SDK import path changes. |

These SDK functions classify a caught error as transient/non-transient or rate-limit/quota by matching a fixed keyword/phrase list against the error message text (e.g. `'ECONNREFUSED'`, `'ENOTFOUND'`, `'timeout'`, `'fetch failed'`, `'rate limit'`, `'quota exceeded'`, `'throttled'`, `'credits depleted'`, plus digit-string matches for `'429'`/`'402'`). This is a genuine decision point, but the source it lives in is an SDK/engine file outside this TUI slice (not one of the 45 files assigned here), so its battery replacement is recorded against the engine's own inventory rather than duplicated here; this test file only pins the fixture behavior. See the final report for a flag on this. |

## Core

| File | Disposition | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `src/core/active-model-identity.ts` | PORT | Resolves what the header/footer show for "which model is serving" by structurally comparing the live provider-registry key against the configured selection; divergence detection is a strict equality/membership check, not a guess. Carries over unchanged. |
| `src/core/approval-alert.ts` | PORT | Fires the unfocused-user desktop/webhook alert the instant a tool call becomes a real permission prompt, wrapping the handler assignment site. All gating is deterministic config/focus reads. |
| `src/core/bookmark-navigation.ts` | PORT | Resolves a bookmark key that missed a direct BlockMeta lookup by parsing the fixed `msg_<idx>` key format and re-resolving through the conversation's own line registry. Fixed-format key parsing, not free-text guessing. |
| `src/core/budget-breach-notifier.ts` | PORT | Edge-triggered (false→true) budget-breach alert with a re-arm latch on threshold change or dropping back under budget. Pure numeric comparison against a configured USD threshold. |
| `src/core/compaction-receipt.ts` | PORT | Pure formatter turning the SDK's typed COMPACTION_RECEIPT payload into the multi-line `[Compaction]` transcript block. No guesswork, just field-by-field rendering. |
| `src/core/composer-state.ts` | PORT | Derives the composer's mode/status/risk labels from the submission-router's typed intent and the live turn-state enum. Structural dispatch over closed enums. |
| `src/core/context-usage.ts` | PORT | Shared tokens/contextWindow ratio arithmetic (raw/clamped ratio, pct, remaining). Pure numeric formula. |
| `src/core/conversation-compaction-render.ts` | PORT | Folds a compaction-continuation user message to one header+preview row. Pure rendering, no guesswork. |
| `src/core/conversation-event-navigation.ts` | PORT | Resolves "jump to next/prev transcript event of kind X" into a buffer line number by filtering/sorting a typed event list. Structural. |
| `src/core/conversation-fold.ts` | PORT | Decides which transcript rows render folded and the blank-line spacing rule that follows them, shared between the renderer and the line cache so the two never disagree. Deterministic predicates over structural flags (collapse state, node kind, depth). |
| `src/core/conversation-line-cache.ts` | PORT | Per-message Line[] memoisation cache keyed on a complete, enumerated input tuple (content signature, width, config, block base, tool-outcome key, turn-structure fields, collapse-state reads). Pure memoisation, byte-identical to a cold render by construction. |
| `src/core/conversation-render-context.ts` | PORT | The render-context shape and its pure derivations (tool-call outcome from a fixed `Error: ` / `Error: cancelled by user` prefix convention, turn-collapse lookup). The `Error: ` prefix is the SDK's own structured tool-result convention (a fixed machine format), not free-text classification. |
| `src/core/conversation-rendering.ts` | PORT | The transcript row renderers (user/assistant/system/tool messages, tool-call nodes, splash screen). `NAVIGABLE_KINDS` dispatches on a closed, already-computed `SystemMessageKind` enum (fixed set membership), not text classification. Pure drawing logic throughout. |
| `src/core/conversation-search-expansion.ts` | PORT | Tracks which collapse keys transcript search auto-expanded so they restore on search close unless the user separately touched them. Two Set-membership bookkeeping structures, no guesswork. |
| `src/core/conversation-splash-state.ts` | PORT | Small state machine for when the splash screen owns the conversation area and the splash→transcript repaint edge. Three booleans and their transitions. |
| `src/core/conversation-turn-structure.ts` | PORT | Builds the structural render plan (turn grouping, tool-call/result placement, tree connector geometry, agent-subtree splicing). Every rule (model-key equality, prose presence, call-id/result matching, JSON-shaped `agentId` extraction) is a structural match over typed fields, not free-text guessing. |
| `src/core/conversation-types.ts` | PORT | Shared TUI extension of the SDK's BlockMeta type (adds render-position fields and the `assistant_turn`/`tool_group` block kinds). Type definitions only. |
| `src/core/conversation-user-receipts.ts` | PORT | Tracks which system-message indices are a direct receipt for an explicit user action, vs ambient boot chatter, via an explicit `isUserReceipt` flag set at the call site. No inference from message text. |
| `src/core/conversation.ts` | PORT | The TUI's ConversationManager subclass: history buffer, block registry, collapse state, splash gating, streaming block updates, undo/redo bookkeeping. All deterministic state management over the SDK base class. |
| `src/core/format-user-error.ts` | HOIST | Engine subsystem: routing. `formatUserFacingError`/`classifyError` turns a caught provider/network error into a plain-language message + suggested action. This is the file intent.md names explicitly ("To engine routing, as a JEV classify reading: the user error formatter"). See decision-points table. Hoisted in E.4 as `packages/engine/sdk/src/platform/routing/user-error.ts` (`readUserFacingError`, `readUserFacingErrorLine`): the messages and actions are unchanged; a 401, a 429 and the connection errno table (on the error or its cause) stay code; the wording is read in one request by the engine failure reading (`engine.failure-reading`) fanned out with `routing.user-error` (has a subscription session ended?). |
| `src/core/long-task-notifier.ts` | PORT | Fires push notifications when a turn/agent task exceeds a configured elapsed-seconds threshold. Pure numeric threshold + focus/config gating; message text is built from structural metadata only. |
| `src/core/memory-diagnostics-gateway.ts` | PORT | Thin RPC wrapper for the daemon's `ops.memory.get` verb. Classifies a rejection as "unavailable" only on the fixed HTTP status codes 501/404 (an explicit fixed-format carve-out, not a decision point), otherwise a generic error. |
| `src/core/memory-provenance.ts` | PORT | Reads the `memory.showProvenance` toggle and extracts the SDK's structural `metadata.memory.recordIds` field from a turn. Defensive field reads, no inference. |
| `src/core/model-routing-chip.ts` | PORT | Turns a MODEL_CHANGED event into a one-line "[Routing] model changed" transcript notice, correlating it to a recent fallback-log entry by provider-id string equality within a time window. Structural correlation (id equality + timestamp window), not text classification; the reason text itself is quoted verbatim from the fallback log, never classified here. |
| `src/core/pairing-tailscale-gateway.ts` | PORT | Thin RPC wrappers for the daemon's `tailscale.get` / `tailscale.serve.run` verbs, degrading to null on any failure. No guesswork. |
| `src/core/permission-mode.ts` | HOIST | Engine subsystem: gate. Owns the `permissions.mode` value vocabulary (`prompt`/`allow-all`/`custom`/`plan`/`accept-edits`), its user-facing labels/tones, and the Shift+Tab cycle order. Per intent's platform table ("permission modes become presets over a stakes table") and the TUI section ("the TUI keeps the cards"), this vocabulary and cycle logic is exactly what becomes the engine gate's new preset table; the product's Shift+Tab key, `/plan` toggle, and footer pill call the gate for the preset list and the next-in-cycle preset instead of owning this table themselves. Purely deterministic enum/cycle logic (no decision point) both before and after the move. |
| `src/core/power-chip-source.ts` | PORT | Topology-aware source for the "sleep disabled" chip: reads the in-process PowerManager directly (embedded topology) or polls the daemon's `power.status.get` (adopted-daemon topology). Deterministic branching on a boolean topology flag. |
| `src/core/power-status.ts` | PORT | Flattens the SDK's PowerState into the surface projection and renders the honest status lines. Pure structural mapping. |
| `src/core/rewind-receipt.ts` | PORT | Pure formatter turning the SDK's RewindReceipt into the multi-line `[Rewind]` transcript block. No guesswork. |
| `src/core/scriptable-statusline.ts` | PORT | Runs the user's configured shell command at turn boundaries and sanitizes its stdout to one safe line (ANSI/control-char stripping, length cap). Fixed-format sanitization of a shell command's own output, not natural-language classification. |
| `src/core/session-auto-titler.ts` | HOIST | Engine subsystem: generation (intent's own words: "To engine generation: the session auto-titler"). Generates a short session title from the first user message via a weak-model chat call. See decision-points table. |
| `src/core/session-resume-core.ts` | PORT | The one canonical resume sequence (load, reset+restore conversation, restore rewind anchors, replay journal, reselect model, reopen panels with modal-redirect skip). All deterministic sequencing and structural checks. |
| `src/core/stream-event-wiring.ts` | PORT | Wires STREAM_*/TOOL_* runtime events to live metrics and drives the optimizer-gated failover retry path. The failover candidate pick ("first capable, unvisited, non-synthetic node in the fallback chain") is a structural filter over typed fields, not a guess; the actual error-message classification and provider-setup classification it calls are hoisted decision points living in other files (`format-user-error.ts`, `../providers/provider-classification.ts`), not in this file. |
| `src/core/stream-stall-watchdog.ts` | PORT | Detects no-delta silence gaps longer than a threshold and emits one hint per episode. Pure timer/state-machine logic. |
| `src/core/system-message-noise.ts` | PORT | Classifies a system message as emit/drop/fold by matching FIXED, product-owned message-prefix formats this same codebase emits (`[Local] ... from last session`, `[Agents] N running:`, `[Replay] … WRFC chain <id> transitioned … waiting for action`). These are known machine-generated tag formats (mirrors the daemon's Discord/Slack `classifyKind` precedent), not free-text human meaning, so this is not a decision point. The one WRFC-referencing rule (suppressing a stale "chain transitioned" replay line for a terminal chain) is general noise-filtering machinery, not WRFC orchestration itself; the "chain" terminology it reads carries forward as the contract tree's own state once the underlying chain-terminal lookup is re-pointed there, a rename, not new guesswork. |
| `src/core/system-message-router.ts` | PORT | Routes a system message to the conversation based on its already-computed kind/priority (computed elsewhere, in the SDK) and a configured target, plus a fixed force-inline prefix list (`[Error]`, `[Failover]`, etc.). No classification happens in this file itself. |
| `src/core/terminal-notifier.ts` | PORT | Emits in-terminal OSC 9 desktop notifications gated by per-signal config toggles and the focus tracker. Deterministic; message text is control-character-stripped and length-capped. |
| `src/core/turn-budget-outcome.ts` | PORT | Renders a turn-budget exhaustion (`failureKind: 'max_turns'`) as an honest budget line, reading the typed limit/source fields the SDK stamps rather than regexing prose. Enum dispatch, no guesswork. |
| `src/core/turn-cancellation.ts` | PORT | Builds the "cancel the active turn" and "cancel the running tool call" actions. Target selection is an honest structural rule (tracked active call, or the sole in-flight call, else refuse), never a guess among several. |
| `src/core/turn-event-wiring.ts` | PORT | Wires TURN_*/TOOL_*/AGENT_*/WORKFLOW_CHAIN_FAILED runtime events to auto-save, journaling, rewind-anchor recording, and the notifier classes defined elsewhere in this slice. Calls `workstreamFailureNotification` (JEV, see that file) but contains no classification of its own. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/core/format-user-error.ts:78-127` (`classifyError`) | A keyword/regex ladder run over a concatenated status-code/name/message/cause-chain probe string: patterns like `invalid.{0,10}(api.?key\|key\|token)`, `rate.?limit`, `too many requests`, `context.{0,10}(length\|window\|limit)`, `econnrefused`, `socket hang up`, `timeout`, etc., matched in a fixed most-specific-first order to bucket the error into `auth` \| `rate-limit` \| `context-overflow` \| `network` \| `generic`. | Hoisted in E.4: `readUserErrorClass` in engine routing/user-error.ts. Status 401 and 429 and errno codes decide in code; everything else is the `engine.failure-reading` categories and yes/no readings (authentication, rate limited or quota, context exceeded, transient network or network/timeout category) plus `routing.user-error` for the subscription-session wording, in one fanned-out request. |
| `src/core/session-auto-titler.ts:~85-100` (`createSessionAutoTitler`'s `model.chat` call) | An ad hoc weak/fast-model chat call, prompted with a hand-written system prompt ("You write terse chat titles… Reply with ONLY a 3 to 6 word title…"), whose freeform reply is then cleaned by `sanitizeTitle`'s own regex/truncation heuristics (strip quotes, strip trailing punctuation, length-cap at 60 chars). | Engine generation reading (judged-output-against-goal style): produce a typed, length-bounded session title from the first user message, in the engine's generation subsystem, replacing the raw prompt-and-string-clean with a proper generation call whose output is already well-formed. |
| `src/test/core/adaptive-planner.test.ts` (whole file, pinning `AdaptivePlanner.select()`) | A hand-tuned weighted-score formula and named numeric thresholds picking an execution strategy from numeric/boolean signals: `riskScore > 0.7` → `HIGH_RISK_SINGLE_PREFERRED` (forces `single`), `latencyBudgetMs < 5000` → `LOW_LATENCY_SINGLE`, `backgroundEligible` + low risk → `background`, `remoteAvailable` + low risk → `remote` (score `65 + (1-risk)*15` vs `single`'s `50+10`), else a multi-step task scores `cohort`. | Choice-over-fixed-options / score-on-a-rubric battery (per the platform table's "core" row: "planner risk is a score"): a single Jev reading choosing among {single, cohort, background, remote} scored on a stakes/latency/risk rubric, replacing the hand-tuned weighted formula and its named thresholds. This test's assertions will need to target the new battery's choices rather than the current threshold values. |
| `src/core/format-user-error.ts:78-127` (`classifyError`, exercised by `format-user-error.test.ts`) | A keyword/regex ladder run over a concatenated status-code/name/message/cause-chain probe string (patterns like `invalid.{0,10}(api.?key\|key\|token)`, `rate.?limit`, `context.{0,10}(length\|window\|limit)`, `econnrefused`, `timeout`, etc.), matched most-specific-first to bucket the error into `auth` \| `rate-limit` \| `context-overflow` \| `network` \| `generic`. | Hoisted in E.4: `readUserErrorClass` in engine routing/user-error.ts. Status 401 and 429 and errno codes decide in code; everything else is the `engine.failure-reading` categories and yes/no readings (authentication, rate limited or quota, context exceeded, transient network or network/timeout category) plus `routing.user-error` for the subscription-session wording, in one fanned-out request. |
| `src/core/session-auto-titler.ts` (`createSessionAutoTitler`'s `model.chat` call, exercised by `session-auto-titler.test.ts`) | An ad hoc weak/fast-model chat call prompted with a hand-written system prompt ("You write terse chat titles... Reply with ONLY a 3 to 6 word title..."), whose freeform reply is then cleaned by `sanitizeTitle`'s own regex/truncation heuristics (strip quotes, strip trailing punctuation, length-cap at 60 chars). | Engine generation reading (judged-output-against-goal style): produce a typed, length-bounded session title from the first user message, in the engine's generation subsystem, replacing the raw prompt-and-string-clean with a proper generation call whose output is already well-formed. |
| `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/core` `classifyIntent` (exercised by `intent-classifier.test.ts`) | A hand-tuned signal-count heuristic over free text: counts named signals (`question_word`, `short_no_action`, `action_verb`, `file_references`, `multiple_deliverables`, `parallelism_keywords`, `multi_sentence_actions`, `spec_plan_reference`, `long_message`, `documentation_request`, matched via keyword/phrase/regex rules) and applies fixed thresholds (3+ signals -> `project`, confidence > 0.65 named as a pass bar) to bucket a message into `chat` \| `task` \| `project`. | Choice-over-fixed-options battery: "does this message need chat, a single task, or project-scope multi-step work", replacing the keyword-signal-count-and-threshold ladder, per intent's core row "intent is a choice". |
| `@pellux/goodvibes-sdk/platform/core` `prepareConversationForTurn` (exercised by `orchestrator-turn-helpers.test.ts`) | Decides whether to inject a "[Project mode]" system message by reading the same kind of free-text signals as `classifyIntent` (a long single-focused-task prompt does not trigger it; a prompt naming architecture, phases, multiple deliverables and an execution plan does). | Same choice-over-fixed-options battery as `classifyIntent` (chat/task/project), reused here to gate project-mode system-message injection instead of a bespoke duplicate heuristic. |

Pure wiring; the decision points and JEV/HOIST dispositions live in the individual surface files this composes, not here. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/panels/project-planning-answer-actions.ts:47-51` | `buildAnswerActions` matches the free-text `question.prompt` (lower-cased) against fixed substring lists to decide whether the question is about scope ('scope', 'in or out'), tasks ('task', 'dependency', 'work breakdown'), verification ('verification', 'test', 'prove'), or approval ('approved', 'approve', 'execution'), and offers the corresponding canned-answer bundle(s) accordingly. | Four yes/no batteries against the contract runner (per intent's explicit "JEV yes/no reading" for this file): "does this planning question require the scope answer bundle", "...the task-breakdown bundle", "...the verification-gates bundle", "...the approval bundle", each yes/no, replacing the keyword ladder. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/renderer/system-message.ts:11-67` | `classifySystemMessage` matches keyword/phrase lists and regexes against a system-message string's free text to sort it into error/warning/info: generic-message keyword lists (`\b(error\|failed\|denied\|crash\w*\|exception)\b`, `\b(warning\|context usage\|caution\|deprecated)\b`), plus prefix-specific keyword checks inside `[WRFC]`/`[Agents]`/`[Model]`/`[Recovery]`-tagged messages (`FAILED`, `cascade abort`, `spawning a fix agent`, `Unknown model`, `Failed to restore`), with a quote-stripping step added specifically to reduce false positives from task descriptions that happen to contain words like "error". | Choice-over-fixed-options battery: classify this system status message as error / warning / info. |
| `src/test/renderer/system-message.test.ts:6-138` | Pins `classifySystemMessage`'s keyword/phrase-list and regex classification of a system-message string into error/warning/info: generic-message keyword lists (`error`, `failed`, `denied`, `crash`, `exception`, `warning`, `context usage`, `caution`, `deprecated`), prefix-specific keyword checks inside `[WRFC]`/`[Agents]`/`[Model]`/`[Recovery]`-tagged messages (`FAILED`, `cascade abort`, `spawning a fix agent`, `Cohort complete: N failed`, `Unknown model`, `Failed to restore`), and a quote-stripping step to suppress false positives from quoted task descriptions containing "error". | Choice-over-fixed-options battery: classify this system status message as error / warning / info. |

| `src/runtime/bootstrap-command-context.ts` | PORT | Assembles the slash-command `CommandContext` from the composed services (session, provider, workspace, platform, ops, extensions, clients sections). Pure dependency wiring, no guesswork. |
| `src/runtime/bootstrap-command-parts.ts` | PORT | The per-section builder functions (`createBootstrapCommandSessionSection` etc.) and the model-selection action handler. Deterministic construction and a fixed-target dispatch (main/helper/tool/tts) over an enum, not a guess. |
| `src/runtime/bootstrap-core.ts` | JEV | Explicit owner override: this file's `WrfcPreRouterBuffer` class and its `routeOrBuffer`-driven subscriptions to `WORKFLOW_CONSTRAINTS_ENUMERATED` / `WORKFLOW_FIX_ATTEMPTED` / `WORKFLOW_REVIEW_COMPLETED` (plus the WRFC agent-guard trace routing) turn WRFC chain/workflow events into operator system messages. Per the standing ruling, a file that manages WRFC-era state for display is not a plain PORT: it becomes a notification over the contract tree (the same messages, sourced from unit/group progress instead of WRFC chain events). No natural-language guessing is involved in this switch. The rest of the file (session bootstrap, provider wiring, hook bridge registration, webhook notifier attach) is ordinary composition-root wiring and carries over unchanged. |
| `src/runtime/bootstrap-hook-bridge.ts` | PORT | `createResumeSessionHandler`: the session-resume seam (multi-instance confirm, delegates the mechanical restore to `resumeSessionCore`, session-spine mirror, hook fire). No WRFC/QEMU content, no guesswork. |

Its QEMU-specific scaffold/bootstrap functions (SSH key generation, cloud-init templates, guest-bundle/manifest read-write) become dead weight once QEMU sandboxing is fully removed at the engine layer, but that is a follow-on trim during the hoist, not a reason to split this file's disposition. ### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/test/runtime/compaction/quality-score.test.ts` (pinning the SDK's `computeQualityScore`/`LOW_QUALITY_THRESHOLD`, imported via `@/runtime/index.ts`) | A hand-tuned numeric threshold (`LOW_QUALITY_THRESHOLD`) applied to a blended compression-ratio-plus-signal score to grade a compaction's output A-F and decide `isLowQuality`/whether to escalate strategy. | fidelity check pattern: judge how well the compacted handoff preserved what mattered against the original conversation, scored on a rubric, per the intent's own "compaction quality uses the fidelity pattern" note for this subsystem. |
| `src/test/runtime/forensics/forensics.test.ts` (pinning the SDK's `classifyFailure`, imported via `@/runtime/index.ts`) | A keyword/phrase search over a turn or task's free-text error message ("timeout" -> `turn_timeout`; "rate limit", "overloaded", "503" -> `llm_error`) to choose among 9 failure categories, layered under priority rules for the structured signals (cancelled, compaction error, permission denial, tool failure, cascade). | coarsen pattern: classify the failure's error text into the closed set of failure categories (cancelled / max_tokens / compaction_error / permission_denied / tool_failure / cascade_failure / turn_timeout / llm_error / unknown) with a single reading, keeping the already-structured signals (cancelled, has-tool-failure, etc.) as inputs the reading is given rather than separate keyword lookups. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/input/commands/schedule-nl.ts:67` | `parseNaturalLanguageSchedule` turns a free-text scheduling phrase ("every weekday at 9am", "in 2 hours", "daily", "every monday at 08:00") into a cron/every/at schedule via a hand-written ladder of regexes and keyword/weekday-name lookup tables (`DOW`, `UNIT_MS`, the `every`/`in`/`at` phrase matchers). | JEV date-parts reading: extract the schedule's structured parts (kind: cron/every/at, plus the numeric interval/time-of-day/weekday components) from the natural-language phrase, per intent's "To the engine scheduler, as a JEV date-parts reading." |
| `src/input/commands/teamwork-runtime.ts:101` | `classifyArchetype` lowercases an agent archetype's name+description into a haystack string and matches keyword substrings ("review"/"audit" → review; "test" or has exec+write-without-edit → test; "research"/"analysis" or has find+analyze-without-write/edit → research; has write or edit → implement; else general) to decide the archetype's family, which in turn sets its `reviewMode` (`'wrfc'` only for the review family). | Choice-over-options battery: which family (review, test, research, implement, or general) best describes this agent archetype, given its name, description, and declared tools. |
| `src/input/commands/planning-runtime.ts:228` | The `/project-plan answer <question> <answer>` subcommand submits a free-text answer to an open planning question via `projectPlanningService.answerQuestion(...)`, whose evaluation of whether the answer resolves the open question is exactly the site intent names. | JEV yes/no reading, per intent's own naming: "To the contract runner, as a JEV yes/no reading: project planning answer actions": does this answer resolve the open planning question, yes/no. |
| `src/test/input/planning-runtime-command.test.ts` (exercising `src/input/commands/planning-runtime.ts:228`) | The `/project-plan answer <question> <answer>` subcommand submits a free-text answer to an open planning question via `projectPlanningService.answerQuestion(...)`, whose evaluation of whether the answer resolves the open question is exactly the site intent names. | JEV yes/no reading, per intent's own naming: "To the contract runner, as a JEV yes/no reading: project planning answer actions": does this answer resolve the open planning question, yes/no. |
| `src/test/input/schedule-nl.test.ts` (exercising `src/input/commands/schedule-nl.ts:67`) | `parseNaturalLanguageSchedule` turns a free-text scheduling phrase ("every weekday at 9am", "in 2 hours", "daily", "every monday at 08:00") into a cron/every/at schedule via a hand-written ladder of regexes and keyword/weekday-name lookup tables. | JEV date-parts reading: extract the schedule's structured parts (kind: cron/every/at, plus the numeric interval/time-of-day/weekday components) from the natural-language phrase, per intent's "To the engine scheduler, as a JEV date-parts reading." |
| `src/test/input/product-breadth-commands.test.ts` (exercising `src/input/commands/teamwork-runtime.ts:101`, same site as `teamwork-runtime.test.ts`) | The test's `/teamwork archetypes` assertion drives a real custom agent-definition file (description "Documentation specialist for operator handoff notes") through `classifyArchetype`, which lowercases the archetype's name+description into a haystack and matches keyword substrings ("review"/"audit" → review; "test" → test; "research"/"analysis" → research; has write or edit → implement; else general), and pins the literal expected output `implement`. | Choice-over-options battery: which family (review, test, research, implement, or general) best describes this agent archetype, given its name, description, and declared tools. |


## Test infrastructure and cross-cutting tests

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/test/agents/context-window.test.ts:145-183` (`isContextSizeExceededError`, `platform/types`) | Matches an arbitrary provider error's message text against a list of phrases ("context_length_exceeded", "context size exceeded", "context window exceeded", "prompt is too long", "input too long", "tokens exceed") to decide whether the failure means the model's context window was exceeded. | Yes/no battery: "does this provider error mean the model's context window was exceeded". |
| `src/test/agents/score-extraction.test.ts:13-88` (`extractScoreFromText`, `platform/agents`) | Regex extraction of a numeric score (e.g. "Score: 8/10", "scored 8.5", "rated 7") from a reviewer's freeform prose. | Score-on-a-rubric battery: score the reviewed work directly, no text to parse. |
| `src/test/agents/score-extraction.test.ts:94-133` (`extractPassedFromText`, `platform/agents`) | Keyword match ("passed"/"approved"/"failed"/"passes"/"passing") over freeform review prose, combined with a score threshold, to decide pass/fail. | Judge-output-against-goal battery: does this attempt satisfy the goal, yes/no. |
| `src/test/agents/score-extraction.test.ts:139-190` (`extractIssuesFromText`, `platform/agents`) | Regex/bracket parsing of a freeform numbered or bulleted list ("1. [critical] ...", "- (major) ...") to extract severity-tagged issues from review prose. | Policy-checklist / score-on-a-rubric battery: return structured findings per dimension directly, no text to parse. |
| `src/test/integrations/github.test.ts:262-291` (`GitHubIntegration.eventToPrompt`, `platform/integrations`, issue_comment branch) | Checks whether an issue comment's body contains the literal substring "@goodvibes" to decide whether the comment addresses the assistant and should become an agent prompt. | Existence-check / yes-no battery: "does this comment address or summon the assistant". |
| `src/test/release-gates/operability-gate.test.ts:76-96,139-140` | `classifyFailure` maps a free-text `errorMessage` string ("request timed out after 30s", "API error 503 service unavailable", "network failure: ECONNRESET") to a `FailureClass` category by substring matching, alongside its structural boolean/enum cases. | Choice-over-fixed-options battery / routing pattern: which failure class (timeout, LLM/provider error, tool failure, etc.) does this error message describe, over the closed `FailureClass` set. |
| `src/test/release-gates/performance-gate.test.ts:177-186` | `computeQualityScore` computes a hand-tuned numeric score and applies `LOW_QUALITY_THRESHOLD` (0.4) over compaction input/output signals (token counts, handoff presence, message-count sanity) to decide `isLowQuality` and a letter grade. | Fidelity check pattern (named directly by the intent document: "compaction quality uses the fidelity pattern"). |
| `src/test/tools/registry-tool.test.ts:207-219` | The `recommend` mode's ranking sorts skills/tools by keyword overlap between a free-text `task` description (e.g. "review code quality") and each candidate's name/description, to decide display order. | Rerank pattern (named directly by the intent document for the skills subsystem: "relevance by rerank"). |

| `docs/share-command.md` | PORT | Reference doc for `/share` session export, including its fixed-pattern secret-redaction table (API-key/token prefixes, home-directory paths). Fixed-format credential redaction, a deterministic-boundary security check, not a decision point. |
| `docs/tools-and-commands.md` | PORT | Reference doc for built-in tools, slash-command families, keybindings, agent archetypes, hooks, and MCP integration. Pure prose/tables. |
| `docs/verification/local-verification.md` | PORT | Reference doc for the three verification layers (local signal, local behavior, external outcome) and the ledger/audit/live-verifier commands. Pure prose/tables. |
| `docs/voice-and-live-tts.md` | PORT | Reference doc for `/tts` live playback, TTS provider/voice configuration, and the markdown-to-speech stripping table. Fixed-format text transform reference, not natural-language judgment. |
| `docs/windows.md` | PORT | Reference doc for Windows support status (WSL2 today, native Windows beta blockers). Pure prose/tables. |
| `docs/wrfc/wrfc-a7c9e318-engineer-report.json` | DROP | A literal WRFC-era generated artifact: an actual persisted engineer-report run output (summary, gatheredContext, plannedActions, appliedChanges, filesCreated/Modified, decisions, issues) for chain `wrfc-a7c9e318`, checked in as WRFC chain-run evidence. This is WRFC run-output data itself, not prose explaining WRFC's history, so it falls squarely in the WRFC-specific generated-artifact DROP carve-out. |

| `examples/reference-node-host/README.md` | PORT | Usage doc for the reference node-host client: pairing/heartbeat/work-pull lifecycle, built-in work-type handlers, command allowlisting. Pure prose. |
| `examples/reference-node-host/src/cli.ts` | PORT | CLI entry point for the reference node-host example: argv parsing by exact flag/subcommand matching (`--config`, `contract`/`pair`/`verify`/`once`/`start`) and JSON config loading. Structural command dispatch over a fixed, closed set of subcommands, not natural-language guesswork. |
| `examples/reference-node-host/src/index.ts` | PORT | Barrel export for the reference node-host client. Matches the daemon precedent: published entry points and reference material are PORT. |
| `examples/reference-node-host/src/reference-node-host.ts` | PORT | Reference implementation of a distributed node-host client (pairing, heartbeat, work pull/complete) demonstrating the SDK's remote-peer surface. Every branch is a closed-set `work.type` dispatch (`status.request`/`location.request`/`session.message`/`automation.run`/`invoke`) and an exact command-allowlist membership check, no free-text classification. |

| `examples/reference-operator-client/README.md` | PORT | Documents the reference operator client example. |

| `.github/workflows/action-self-test.yml` | PORT | Non-gating workflow that installs the published GitHub Action wrapper and exercises `doctor --json`; fixed job graph, exit-code contract explicitly not masked. |
| `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | PORT | Main CI pipeline: typecheck, test, coverage gate, architecture check, perf check, eval gate, build+smoke, package gate, and an auto-release job that tags and dispatches release.yml on green main. All fixed job graph and shell conditionals. |
| `.github/workflows/release.yml` | PORT | Release pipeline: tag-version verification, by-reference per-job-green re-verification, binary matrix, GH release, staged npm/GitHub Packages publish with kill switches. Fixed job graph. |
| `.github/workflows/windows-beta.yml` | PORT | Non-gating beta Windows build+smoke workflow, weekly and on-demand. Fixed job graph. |

| `.goodvibes/agents/reviewer.md` | JEV | JEV in the contract runner (docs/inventory/wrfc-to-jev.md, docs/design/contract-runner.md): the reviewer rubric's evidence rules become the evidence the runner collects for each unit and the judge's criteria; the file is rewritten as the runner's evidence guidance. |
| `.goodvibes/eval/baseline.json` | PORT | Eval-suite score baseline (`gate:*` scenario scores) consumed by `scripts/eval-gate.ts`'s regression gate. Not WRFC-related; plain scenario-scoring data, not a coverage-percentage gate (not Supercov). |
| `.goodvibes/GOODVIBES.md` | PORT | This repo's own AI-assistant working-style directives (token-usage minimization, batching, multi-agent execution). One bullet ("NEVER skip WRFC without explicit user confirmation") mentions WRFC in passing among many unrelated directives; the file's content and purpose are not specifically about WRFC as its operating procedure, so the WRFC carve-out does not apply. Carries over as this project's own tooling configuration. |
| `.goodvibes/.npmignore` | PORT | Deliberately-empty file that pins `.goodvibes/`'s npm packing to package.json's `files` allowlist regardless of a local `.goodvibes/.gitignore`. Packaging mechanics only. |
| `.goodvibes/skills/add-provider/SKILL.md` | PORT | A shipped agent skill document walking a user through adding a custom LLM provider (conversational field collection, validation rules, JSON schema, smart defaults for a fixed list of known provider names). The "smart defaults" step matches a user-typed name against a closed, enumerated set of known providers (ollama/together/openrouter/groq/lm-studio/fireworks/vllm), an exact/enum match, not a free-text classification heuristic; the surrounding conversational collection is delegated to the agent's own judgment already, not a hand-tuned heuristic in code. No WRFC/QEMU content. |

| `scripts/assemble-platform-packages.ts` | PORT | Release-time step copying built binaries + sqlite-vec addon into each platform package's `bin/` and stamping its version. Deterministic file/version mechanics. |
| `scripts/audit-goodvibes-home.ts` | PORT | CLI wrapper around the SDK's `.goodvibes` home audit, rendering Markdown/JSON. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/bun-compile-compat.ts` | PORT | Prebuild step patching known Bun-compile incompatibilities in vendored deps via exact source-string replacement. |
| `scripts/check-architecture.ts` | PORT | CI static-analysis gate: line-count cap, pattern-based rules over code syntax, explicit-`any` detection via the TS compiler API, import-cycle detection (Tarjan SCC), layer-boundary rules, hex-literal/selected-index/unused-export/internal-identifier ratchets, and SDK contract-catalog invariants. Every rule matches source-code shape or a fixed machine grammar, never free-text meaning; matches the daemon's `check-architecture.ts` precedent exactly. |
| `scripts/check-bun.sh` | PORT | `preinstall` guard: refuses install when `bun` is not on PATH. |
| `scripts/check-workflows.ts` | PORT | Structural validation of GitHub Actions workflow YAML (parses, checks required fields, bans job-level `continue-on-error`, checks release-workflow job requirements). Structural/schema checks over YAML keys, not free text. |
| `scripts/coverage-gate.ts` | PORT | Aggregate whole-suite coverage gate, a thin adapter over the shared toolchain's coverage-summary parser and floor comparison, sourced from `toolchain.config.json`. A plain bun-test coverage-percentage gate ported from the daemon; not the named external Supercov tool, so the "Supercov is not part of this project" rule does not apply. |
| `scripts/eval-gate.ts` | PORT | CI eval gate: runs the standing-gate scenario suites, compares against a stored baseline, fails on regression beyond threshold or an absolute floor. Scenario-scoring regression gate, not a coverage-percentage gate; unrelated to Supercov. |
| `scripts/export-foundation-artifacts.ts` | PORT | Thin entry point invoking `syncFoundationArtifacts` under the workspace lock. |
| `scripts/generate-command-reference.ts` | PORT | Regenerates `docs/commands-reference.md` from the live slash-command registry. |
| `scripts/hex-literal-baseline.json` | PORT | Seeded per-file ratchet baseline (allowed raw hex-color-literal counts) consumed by `hex-literal-rule.ts`. Pure data table. (One entry names `src/panels/wrfc-panel-format.ts`, a file outside this slice whose own disposition is handled elsewhere; this baseline file itself is generic ratchet data.) |
| `scripts/hex-literal-rule.ts` | PORT | Architecture-gate rule banning raw hex color literals outside the theme-token source files, enforced as a ratchet against the seeded baseline. Exact regex over a fixed machine syntax (hex color codes), not free-text classification. |
| `scripts/install.sh` | PORT | The public one-line suite installer (`curl \| sh`): resolves per-repository release tags, downloads and checksum-verifies binaries for the daemon/TUI/agent/webui, manages systemd/launchd service units by exact unit-provenance markers, and detects/removes PATH-shadowing copies by an exact `"<command> <dotted-version>"` output-shape match. Every judgment call in it is explicitly built to refuse rather than guess (the file's own comments repeatedly say "no guess," "recognizably platform-managed," "exact shape"); all checks are structural/exact-match over machine state, not natural-language meaning. |
| `scripts/internal-identifier-rule.ts` | PORT | Architecture-gate rule banning internal planning-identifier shorthand (wave/work-order/debt/UX ids, lettered finding ids) from tracked text via a fixed set of regexes over exact machine-defined token shapes, not prose meaning. |
| `scripts/leak-scan.ts` | PORT | Runs the whole suite in one process with a timer-leak detector preloaded, reporting still-live handles. Deterministic harness. |
| `scripts/no-unused-exports-rule.ts` | PORT | Architecture-gate rule banning renderer exports with no non-test import site, via TypeScript AST parsing and cross-file import-binding resolution. Structural analysis, not free-text classification. |
| `scripts/package-install-check.ts` | PORT | Static "as it would install" verification: shared tarball/bin-shim policy (toolchain) plus TUI-specific bin-fallback verification. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/perf-baseline.json` | PORT | Ratchet perf budgets (startup/frame/line-production timings) consumed by `perf-check.ts`. Pure data. |
| `scripts/perf-check.ts` | PORT | CI performance-budget gate: headless startup/frame/line-production measurements compared against committed ratchet budgets. Numeric threshold comparisons over measured timings, not text classification. |
| `scripts/perf-coalesce-bench.ts` | PORT | Headless before/after measurement harness for render-call coalescing. Not a gate. |
| `scripts/perf-frame-bench.ts` | PORT | Shared headless Compositor.composite() throughput benchmark, consumed by both the gate and a release-gate test. |
| `scripts/perf-line-bench.ts` | PORT | Headless line-production micro-benchmarks (transcript build/append/resize, panel build, markdown/code-block/overlay render) with GC-bracketed heap accounting. Pure timing/allocation measurement. |
| `scripts/platform-packages.ts` | PORT | Shared descriptor table for the four `@pellux/goodvibes-tui-<os>-<arch>` platform packages, consumed by assemble/publish/sync scripts. Pure data. |
| `scripts/postinstall.d.ts` | PORT | Type declarations for the plain-JS `postinstall.js`, re-exported from the runtime module it wraps so the two can never disagree. |
| `scripts/postinstall.js` | PORT | npm postinstall: installs the platform binary (from the matching optional platform package, or a checksum-verified download), deploys bundled `.goodvibes` skills/agents/GOODVIBES.md to the user's home on first install (skip-if-exists), and provisions the wake-word model (never fails the install). Deterministic file/network mechanics; the "deploy bundled files" step would stop copying `.goodvibes/agents/reviewer.md` once that file is dropped from the source tree, but this script's own logic needs no change. |
| `scripts/prebuild.ts` | PORT | Prebuild entry point: patches Bun-compile compatibility and syncs project surfaces under the workspace lock. |
| `scripts/project-surfaces.ts` | PORT | Syncs version strings into `src/version.ts`/README, regenerates foundation artifacts (operator/peer contract JSON, knowledge GraphQL/SQL schema) and the command-reference doc from the live SDK/command registry. Deterministic generation and exact regex substitution on fixed string shapes (a version number, a badge URL), not natural-language classification. |
| `scripts/publish-check.ts` | PORT | Pre-publish gate: shared SDK-pin tri-agreement (toolchain), TUI-specific required package.json fields, shared tarball/bin-shim policy, and a registry-auth probe. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/publish-package.ts` | PORT | Stages and publishes the main npm package (with idempotent re-publish handling and optional registry-scope rewriting for the GitHub Packages mirror). Deterministic. |
| `scripts/publish-platform-packages.ts` | PORT | Publishes the assembled platform binary packages, idempotently, with optional scope rescoping for a mirror registry. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/release-gate.ts` | PORT | Runs the five release-gate test suites (safety/determinism/performance/operability/product) and reports pass/fail per gate. Thin test-suite orchestrator, no decision points. |
| `scripts/release.ts` | PORT | Local release-cut wrapper: assembles the CHANGELOG body (from an explicit release-notes env var, refusing raw commit-hash lines via a fixed-shape guard, or falling back to the git log for dry runs) and invokes the shared release-cut tool. The commit-hash-line check is a fixed-shape guard against a known failure mode, not a natural-language judgment about note quality (same reasoning as the daemon's `release.ts`). |
| `scripts/run-tests.ts` | PORT | Parallel per-file test runner: per-file TMPDIR isolation, stale-tmp sweeps, optional substring pattern filter, per-file timeout ceiling. Deterministic orchestration. |
| `scripts/sdk-dev.ts` | PORT | Thin forwarder to the canonical SDK-overlay dev tool in the sibling SDK checkout. |
| `scripts/selected-index-rule.ts` | PORT | Architecture-gate rule banning the raw `[this.selectedIndex]` token in panel source (a real bug class: indexing the raw item array instead of the filtered visible list) via exact-string search over source syntax. Not free-text classification. |
| `scripts/stale-tmp-sweep.ts` | PORT | Age-gated cleanup of stale test-scratch directories under `.test-tmp` and, by an exact enumerated prefix list, the real OS temp directory. Exact-prefix matching, not a heuristic guess. |
| `scripts/sync-platform-optional-deps.ts` | PORT | Release-time step re-stamping the root package's platform `optionalDependencies` versions to match the bumped root version. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/test-pattern-rule.ts` | PORT | Pure argv-parsing helper extracting the optional test-file substring filter, skipping the runner's own known flags. |
| `scripts/test-temp-manifest.ts` | PORT | Handover mechanism between a finished test process and the runner: reads/writes a JSON manifest of temp directories a child owned so the parent can remove them after exit. |
| `scripts/typecheck-coverage-rule.ts` | PORT | Gate rule asserting every tracked TypeScript file is resolved by some checked tsconfig project, via `tsc --listFilesOnly`. Structural, not free-text. |
| `scripts/verification-ledger.ts` | PORT | CLI wrapper rendering the verification ledger as Markdown/JSON. |
| `scripts/verify-live.ts` | PORT | CLI wrapper building and rendering the live-verification report against a running daemon/binary. |
| `scripts/verify-release-tag-version.ts` | PORT | Verifies the pushed/dispatched git tag equals `v${package.json version}`; exact string comparison, ported from the daemon's script of the same name. |
| `scripts/workspace-lock.ts` | PORT | File-based mutual-exclusion lock with staleness detection by age and by checking whether the owning pid is still alive. Deterministic. |
| `toolchain.config.json` | PORT | Shared-toolchain configuration: build targets, coverage floors, publish policy, release-cut settings, smoke-check strings. Pure data. |
| `tsconfig.json` | PORT | TypeScript compiler configuration for the main source tree. |
| `tsconfig.test.json` | PORT | TypeScript compiler configuration for the test tree. |
